{"id":"ed763dd4-00c3-43dc-b47d-1c0188f3bb15","identifier":"DUAL-19","title":"Command routing and shell integration","description":"## Description\n\nImplement `dual run` which routes commands to the workspace's container via `docker exec`, and the shell integration layer that makes this transparent.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **shell-interception**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — Functions work for interactive + Claude Code. Absolute paths bypass (acceptable). Needs rc injection + PATH shims.\n* **shell-interception-transparency**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — Most leaks fixable (hostname, user, HOME, mounts). Platform (`linux` vs `darwin`) unfixable but acceptable.\n* **command-routing-accuracy**: CONFIRMED — Classification is architecturally sound. Separates credential, dependency, and network requirements correctly.\n* **tty-passthrough**: CONFIRMED — `-t` flag allocates pseudo-TTY. TTY detection works in shell functions.\n* **docker-exec-exitcodes**: CONFIRMED — Exit codes 0-255 preserved exactly.\n\n## Requirements\n\n### Explicit routing\n\n`dual run <command>` executes `<command>` inside the current workspace's container:\n\n```bash\ndual run pnpm dev              → docker exec dual-lightfast-feat-auth pnpm dev\ndual run curl localhost:3000   → docker exec dual-lightfast-feat-auth curl localhost:3000\n```\n\nCurrent workspace determined by: current directory (matched to workspace path) or explicit `--workspace` flag.\n\n### Transparent shell integration\n\nThe shell pane in the tmux session runs a Dual-managed shell where runtime commands are automatically routed to the container. Claude Code and the developer never need to type `dual run`.\n\n#### Routing heuristic\n\n* **Container (via** `docker exec`): `pnpm`, `npm`, `npx`, `node`, `yarn`, `bun`, `python`, `pip`, `uv`, `pytest`, `curl`, `wget`, `playwright`, `cypress`\n* **Host (direct execution)**: `git`, `cat`, `ls`, `pwd`, `cd`, `vim`, `nvim`, `claude`, `grep`, `find`, `head`, `tail`, `less`, `wc`\n* **Default for unknown commands**: container (safer — a host command in a container usually works; a container command on the host causes port conflicts)\n\n#### Mechanism\n\nStart with shell wrapper functions (override commands in `.bashrc`/`.zshrc` for workspace shell). Iterate toward a custom shell shim if edge cases demand it.\n\n### Edge cases\n\n* Chained commands: `pnpm install && pnpm dev` — both should run in container\n* Pipes: `curl localhost:3000 | jq .` — curl in container, pipe result to host jq\n* Environment variables: `PORT=3001 pnpm dev` — pass through to container\n* Background processes: `pnpm dev &` — must run in container\n\n## Existing code\n\nExtend `src/shell/` module from Phase 0 (PR #105). Use `std::process::Command` for `docker exec`.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":1,"name":"Urgent"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-19/command-routing-and-shell-integration","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-19-command-routing-and-shell-integration","createdAt":"2026-02-04T09:58:40.795Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:35.539Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:35.516Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["Docker"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{"id":"1e773ab5-087d-4b52-93f3-0d459a906112","identifier":"DUAL-20","title":"Multi-port reverse proxy","description":"## Description\n\nImplement the multi-port reverse proxy that maps `{project}-{branch}.localhost:{port}` to the correct container's ephemeral host port.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **reverse-proxy-subdomain**: CONFIRMED — Host header routing is standard. Custom proxy recommended for dynamic control.\n* **localhost-resolution**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — Works in Chrome, Edge, Firefox. Safari requires macOS 26+. curl works, most CLI tools don't.\n* **websocket-proxy**: CONFIRMED — All major proxies support WebSocket upgrade. Custom proxy must handle explicitly.\n* **sse-proxy**: CONFIRMED — Standard HTTP streaming, all proxies support with proper config (disable buffering).\n* **dynamic-port-registration**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — Custom proxy has full control. Adding new ports may need new listeners.\n* **concurrent-websocket**: CONFIRMED — Standard proxy capability.\n\n## Requirements\n\n### Core proxy behavior\n\n1. Proxy is a Dual subprocess — starts when Dual starts, stops when Dual stops\n2. Listens on all ports that any active container uses (e.g., `:3000`, `:3001`, `:4001`, `:8080`)\n3. Routes based on subdomain in the `Host` header:\n   * Request to `lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3000` → forward to ephemeral host port for that container's `:3000`\n   * Request to `agent-os-main.localhost:8080` → forward to ephemeral host port for that container's `:8080`\n4. Dynamic: as containers start/stop, the routing table and listener ports update\n\n### Protocol support\n\n* HTTP (standard requests)\n* WebSocket (critical for HMR/hot reload) — must handle `Connection: Upgrade` correctly\n* SSE (Server-Sent Events) — must not buffer responses\n* Streaming responses — must not buffer\n\n### Dynamic registration\n\n* When a container starts and Dual knows its port mappings, register routes with the proxy\n* When a container stops, deregister its routes\n* If a port is no longer used by any container, stop listening on it\n* If a new port is needed, start listening on it\n\n### CLI commands\n\n```\ndual urls                         → list all workspace URLs\ndual urls lightfast-feat-auth     → URLs for specific workspace\ndual open                         → open current workspace URLs in browser\ndual open lightfast-main          → open specific workspace URLs in browser\n```\n\n### Fallback\n\n* Request to unknown subdomain → 404 with helpful message listing known workspaces\n* Request without subdomain (bare `localhost:3000`) → 404 with instructions\n\n## Existing code\n\nExtend `src/proxy/` module from Phase 0 (PR #105). Use `hyper` or `axum` for the HTTP proxy layer in Rust.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-20/multi-port-reverse-proxy","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-20-multi-port-reverse-proxy","createdAt":"2026-02-04T09:59:09.120Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:36.921Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:36.903Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["DX","Proxy"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{"id":"a71f3829-5d1b-47ec-a052-030d2069dd1b","identifier":"DUAL-18","title":"Container lifecycle management","description":"## Description\n\nManage Docker container lifecycle as part of workspace creation and destruction. Each workspace gets one container.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **docker-exec-basic**: CONFIRMED — Exit codes preserved. Env vars require `-e`, CWD requires `-w`, TTY requires `-t`.\n* **container-network-isolation**: CONFIRMED — Kernel-level guarantee. Each container has isolated network namespace.\n* **parallel-containers**: CONFIRMED — 7 containers tested successfully.\n* **bind-mount-visibility**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — \\~200ms propagation latency via VirtioFS. Acceptable for hot reload.\n\n## Requirements\n\n### Container creation\n\nWhen a workspace is created (`dual new`), after cloning:\n\n1. Build/reuse the project image (DUAL-17)\n2. `docker create` with:\n   * Image: `dual-{project}:latest`\n   * Name: `dual-{project}-{branch}`\n   * Bind mount: host worktree → `/workspace`\n   * Published ports: ephemeral host ports mapped to known container ports (from DUAL-22 service config)\n   * Working directory: `/workspace`\n3. `docker start` — container runs with `sleep infinity`\n4. `docker exec ... pnpm install` — install dependencies inside container\n\n### Container states\n\n* **Running**: Container is up, ready for `docker exec` commands\n* **Stopped**: Container exists but is stopped. `node_modules` and build cache preserved.\n* **Destroyed**: Container removed. Next access requires full recreation.\n\n### Integration with workspace lifecycle\n\n* `dual new` → clone + create container + start + install deps + create tmux session\n* `dual rm` → destroy tmux session + stop and remove container + deregister ports from proxy + delete clone\n* `dual stop <workspace>` → stop container (preserve state)\n* `dual start <workspace>` → start stopped container\n\n### Container naming\n\n`dual-{project}-{branch}` — e.g., `dual-lightfast-feat-auth`\n\n### Port allocation strategy\n\nEphemeral host port allocation for macOS (container IPs not routable from host):\n\n* Range: `49000–49999` (1000 slots, above the typical ephemeral port range)\n* Allocation: sequential assignment per container, per service port\n* Persistence: port assignments stored in state file. On restart, Dual re-reads and re-applies (browser bookmarks survive restarts)\n* Conflict handling: before assigning a port, check if it's already bound. If so, skip to next available.\n* The proxy (DUAL-20) consumes this mapping to route subdomain requests\n\n### node_modules strategy\n\n`node_modules` lives inside the bind mount. `pnpm install` runs inside the container and writes to `/workspace/node_modules`. Host tools (nvim LSP, Claude Code) can read for type resolution.\n\n## Existing code\n\nExtend `src/container/` module from Phase 0 (PR #105). Use `std::process::Command` for Docker operations.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-18/container-lifecycle-management","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-18-container-lifecycle-management","createdAt":"2026-02-04T09:58:20.870Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:38.359Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:38.341Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["Docker"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{"id":"cf50fa11-b630-49bf-bd12-5fb3adc2799b","identifier":"DUAL-23","title":"Full lifecycle orchestration on workspace select","description":"## Description\n\nWire the fuzzy workspace switcher to the full Docker-aware lifecycle so that selecting any workspace — regardless of current state — triggers the correct sequence to get you into a working session.\n\n## Problem\n\nPhase 0's switcher (DUAL-15) only handles tmux sessions. With the Docker layer, the switcher needs to orchestrate a longer sequence: clone → build image → start container → install deps → create tmux session → attach. Each step may or may not be needed depending on current state.\n\n## Requirements\n\n### State-dependent behavior on workspace selection\n\n| Current state | Actions on select |\n| -- | -- |\n| **ATTACHED** | No-op (already there), or re-focus |\n| **BACKGROUND** | Attach to existing tmux session |\n| **STOPPED** | Start container → create tmux session → attach |\n| **LAZY** | Clone → build/reuse image → create container → start → install deps → create tmux session → attach |\n\n### Progress feedback\n\nThe LAZY → ATTACHED path can take 30+ seconds (clone + image build + pnpm install). The switcher should show progress:\n\n```\nCreating workspace lightfast / feat-auth...\n  ✓ Cloning repository\n  ● Building container image...\n  ○ Installing dependencies\n  ○ Starting session\n```\n\nUse `ratatui` for TUI progress rendering.\n\n### Error handling\n\n* If any step fails, show the error and leave the user in the switcher (don't dump them into a broken session)\n* Partial state should be cleanable: if clone succeeded but container failed, `dual rm` should handle it\n\n### Detach current on switch\n\nWhen switching FROM one workspace TO another:\n\n1. Detach current tmux session (workspace goes to BACKGROUND)\n2. Attach to target workspace's session\n\n## Existing code\n\nIntegration point between the switcher (`src/cli/`), the state machine (DUAL-21), and all Docker lifecycle commands. The orchestration sequence should be a single function that reads current state and executes the needed transitions. `dual new` and the switcher should share the same orchestration code.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":1,"name":"Urgent"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-23/full-lifecycle-orchestration-on-workspace-select","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-23-full-lifecycle-orchestration-on-workspace-select","createdAt":"2026-02-04T10:02:56.445Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:39.876Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:39.850Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["DX","Docker"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{"id":"efbe049c-7db0-41e0-96b2-8108109709fb","identifier":"DUAL-27","title":"End-to-end MVP smoke test","description":"## Description\n\nA single end-to-end test that validates the full Dual MVP loop. Can be automated or manual, but must be tracked and runnable.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **e2e-ci-environment**: CONFIRMED — Docker pre-installed on ubuntu-latest. tmux installable via apt.\n* **e2e-test-isolation**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — UUID naming works for Docker and tmux. RAII cleanup with prefix-based sweep as defense-in-depth.\n* **e2e-local-fixture-repo**: CONFIRMED — `git clone --local` from /tmp works with hardlinks. \\~64ms average.\n\n## The test scenario\n\nStarting from a clean state (no workspaces, no containers):\n\n### 1\\. Config\n\n* Write a `dual.toml` config pointing at a test repo\n* Run `dual ls` — should show no workspaces (or LAZY entries)\n\n### 2\\. Create workspace\n\n* Run `dual new <project> main`\n* Verify: clone exists at the configured workspace directory\n* Verify: container `dual-{project}-main` is running (`docker ps`)\n* Verify: tmux session `dual-{project}-main` exists\n* Verify: attached to the tmux session with correct pane layout\n\n### 3\\. Command routing\n\n* In the shell pane: `pnpm dev` — should start dev server inside container\n* In the shell pane: `git status` — should run on host\n* In the shell pane: `curl localhost:3000` — should hit the dev server inside the container\n\n### 4\\. Reverse proxy\n\n* Run `dual urls` — should show `{project}-main.localhost:3000`\n* Open URL in browser — should show the app\n* Edit a source file — hot reload should fire in the browser\n\n### 5\\. Second workspace\n\n* Create or select a second workspace (e.g., `{project} feat/test`)\n* Verify: second container running, second tmux session created\n* Verify: first workspace is now BACKGROUND (processes still running)\n* Run `dual urls` — should show both workspaces\n* Both accessible simultaneously in browser\n\n### 6\\. Workspace switching\n\n* Switch back to first workspace via fuzzy picker\n* Verify: first is ATTACHED, second is BACKGROUND\n* Both dev servers still running, both browser tabs still working\n\n### 7\\. Cleanup\n\n* `dual rm` both workspaces\n* `docker ps -a | grep dual` — should show nothing\n* Workspace directories should be empty or not exist\n\n## Pass criteria\n\nAll 7 sections complete without manual intervention beyond what's described. No orphaned Docker resources. No port conflicts. Claude Code transparency maintained throughout.\n\n## Existing code\n\nE2E test infrastructure exists from PR #105 (`tests/` directory). Extend with Docker-aware test harness.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-27/end-to-end-mvp-smoke-test","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-27-end-to-end-mvp-smoke-test","createdAt":"2026-02-04T12:46:35.476Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:41.263Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:41.241Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["DX"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{"id":"e38007e7-b364-4b8b-9e42-836fa72a13e2","identifier":"DUAL-22","title":"Project service and port configuration","description":"## Description\n\nExtend the config to declare which ports each project uses and what services they map to. This information drives the reverse proxy's routing table and the `dual urls` / `dual open` commands.\n\n## Progress\n\n**Basic port configuration: DONE** (DUAL-42, PR #112)\n\n* `.dual.toml` per repo now has `ports = [3000, 3001]` (list of u16)\n* `RepoHints` struct includes: `image`, `ports`, `setup`, `env`\n* Consumer modules updated to use hints API\n\n**Remaining: Named services + richer config**\n\n## Requirements (remaining)\n\n### Named service mapping\n\nCurrent `.dual.toml` only stores port numbers. For `dual urls` to show useful output, we need named services:\n\n```toml\n# Current (PR #112):\nports = [3000, 3001, 4001]\n\n# Target:\n[[service]]\nname = \"www\"\nport = 3000\n\n[[service]]\nname = \"app\"\nport = 3001\n\n[[service]]\nname = \"api\"\nport = 4001\n```\n\n### How named services are used\n\n* `dual urls` output: `www  lightfast-main.localhost:3000` instead of just port numbers\n* `dual open` can open specific services: `dual open --service api`\n* Reverse proxy fallback 404 page lists available services by name\n\n### Fallback behavior\n\n* If no `[[service]]` entries but `ports` is set, auto-name as `port-3000`, `port-3001`, etc.\n* If neither is set, default to a single service on `:3000`\n\n### Env file handling\n\nAlso add optional env file warnings:\n\n```toml\nenv_files = [\".env\", \".env.local\"]  # relative to project root\n```\n\nThese files are in the bind-mounted source tree. Dual warns if they're missing on launch.\n\n## Existing code\n\n`src/config.rs` (PR #112) has `RepoHints` with basic `ports: Vec<u16>`. Extend to support named services while keeping backward compatibility with plain port lists.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-22/project-service-and-port-configuration","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-22-project-service-and-port-configuration","createdAt":"2026-02-04T10:02:35.695Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:42.679Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:37:42.662Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["Core"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

Fall back to `git clone <url>` then `git checkout -b` when no local main exists\n\n### Tests\n\n- `test_clone_from_local_args` — verify git clone --local + git checkout -b args\n- `test_clone_fallback_to_remote` — verify fallback when no local main exists"

Keep `write_hints()` for programmatic updates.\n\n### Implementation\n\nAdd `write_default_hints(repo_root)` that writes a well-commented template with examples for all fields (image, ports, setup, env, extra_commands, anonymous_volumes, shared).\n\nUpdate `cmd_add()` in `main.rs` to call `write_default_hints()` instead of `write_hints(&RepoHints::default())`.","projectMilestone":{"id":"f401994e-9d1f-400b-8eed-1ff38fd0866a","name":"v2.1 — Bug Fixes & Completions"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-46/fix-default-dualtoml-hand-write-commented-template","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-46-fix-default-dualtoml-hand-write-commented-template","createdAt":"2026-02-15T10:38:21.521Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:38:21.521Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"dueDate":null,"status":"Backlog","labels":["DX","Core"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

{
  "title": "Configurable container commands via .dual.toml",
  "team": "dual",
  "project": "Dual",
  "priority": 2,
  "labels": [
    "Core",
    "DX"
  ],
  "milestone": "v2.1 — Bug Fixes & Completions",
  "description": "## Problem\n\n`CONTAINER_COMMANDS` at `shell.rs:2-4` is a hardcoded `&[&str]`. Users can't add project-specific commands like `cargo`, `go`, `ruby`.\n\n## Fix\n\nAdd `extra_commands: Vec<String>` field to `RepoHints` with `#[serde(default)]`. Update `shell.rs` to accept additional commands and merge with defaults when generating RC.\n\n### Implementation\n\n1. Add `extra_commands` to `RepoHints` in `config.rs`\n2. Update `write_rc_file()` and `generate_rc()` signatures to accept `extra_commands: &[String]`\n3. Merge `CONTAINER_COMMANDS` with `extra_commands` when generating functions\n\n### Tests\n\n- Test merged command list (defaults + extras)\n- Test RC output contains extra command functions"
}
```

{
  "title": "Configurable anonymous volumes via .dual.toml",
  "team": "dual",
  "project": "Dual",
  "priority": 2,
  "labels": [
    "Docker",
    "Core"
  ],
  "milestone": "v2.1 — Bug Fixes & Completions",
  "description": "## Problem\n\nAnonymous `node_modules` volume is hardcoded at `src/container.rs:156-157`. Users can't add volumes for `.next`, `target`, etc.\n\n## Fix\n\nReplace hardcoded `node_modules` volume with configurable `anonymous_volumes` field in `RepoHints`. Default to `[\"node_modules\"]`.\n\n### Implementation\n\n1. Add `anonymous_volumes: Vec<String>` to `RepoHints` with `#[serde(default)]`\n2. Default impl: `vec![\"node_modules\".to_string()]`\n3. Update `build_create_args()` to iterate `anonymous_volumes` instead of hardcoding\n4. Update `cmd_launch()` to pass `hints.anonymous_volumes`\n\n### Tests\n\n- Test `build_create_args()` with multiple anonymous volumes\n- Test default includes `node_modules`"
}
```

```json
{
  "title": "Context-aware `dual create`: auto-detect repo from cwd",
  "team": "dual",
  "project": "Dual",
  "priority": 2,
  "labels": [
    "Core",
    "DX"
  ],
  "milestone": "v2.2 — Context Awareness & UX",
  "description": "## Problem\n\n`dual create` requires explicit repo name (`dual create myrepo feat/auth`). The `detect_workspace()` function exists (`main.rs:669-688`) but is only used by `cmd_sync()`.\n\n## Fix\n\nMake repo arg optional in `Create` command. When omitted, use `detect_git_repo()` to get current repo info, match against state by git remote URL.\n\n### CLI Change (breaking)\n\n`dual create <repo> <branch>` → `dual create <branch> [--repo REPO]`\n\n### Implementation\n\n1. Update `Create` variant in `cli.rs`: branch as positional, repo as optional `--repo` flag\n2. In `cmd_create()`, if repo is None: detect from cwd, match URL against state\n3. Error with helpful message if not inside a known repo"
}
```

  ],
  "milestone": "v2.2 — Context Awareness & UX",

Users must remember workspace names.\n\n## Fix\n\nMake workspace arg optional for launch, destroy, open, urls commands. When omitted, use `detect_workspace()` to find the current workspace from cwd.\n\n### Implementation\n\n1. Update `Launch` and `Destroy` variants in `cli.rs`: workspace becomes `Option<String>`\n2. When workspace is None, call `detect_workspace()` to find current workspace\n3. Error with helpful message if not inside a known workspace"
}
```

{
  "title": "Detect $TMUX and use switch-client",
  "team": "dual",
  "project": "Dual",
  "priority": 2,
  "labels": [
    "Tmux",
    "DX"
  ],
  "milestone": "v2.2 — Context Awareness & UX",
  "description": "## Problem\n\n`tmux::attach()` (`src/tmux.rs:49-64`) doesn't check `$TMUX` env var. Running `dual launch` from inside tmux creates nested sessions.\n\n## Fix\n\nCheck `$TMUX` env var in `attach()`. If already in tmux, use `tmux switch-client -t <session>` instead of `tmux attach-session -t <session>`.\n\n### Tests\n\n- Test `$TMUX` detection logic\n- Test switch-client args vs attach-session args"
}
```

{
  "title": "Improve `dual list` with grouped repo output",
  "team": "dual",
  "project": "Dual",
  "priority": 3,
  "labels": [
    "DX"
  ],
  "milestone": "v2.2 — Context Awareness & UX",
  "description": "## Problem\n\nCurrent `dual list` output is a flat list. Hard to scan with many workspaces across multiple repos.\n\n## Fix\n\nGroup workspaces by repo in the list output:\n\n```\nlightfast\n  main          ● running    (container: up, tmux: attached)\n  feat/auth     ○ stopped    (container: stopped, tmux: none)\n  feat/billing  ◌ lazy       (not cloned yet)\n\nagent-os\n  main          ● running    (container: up, tmux: background)\n```\n\n### Implementation\n\nUpdate `print_workspace_status()` in `main.rs` to group by repo name and format with indentation."
}
```

{"id":"dd5b4af9-cbf9-4cb6-9d3e-3b0fd4d85f4b","identifier":"DUAL-17","title":"Container image auto-generation","description":"## Description\n\nInspect a monorepo's project files and auto-generate a minimal container image. No Dockerfiles — Dual detects what's needed and builds the image.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **auto-image-generation**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — 80%+ of Node.js projects detectable. Requires fallback for custom system dependencies. ([ARCHITECTURE.md](<http://ARCHITECTURE.md>))\n* **image-caching**: CONFIRMED — Native Docker layer sharing. Must include lockfile hash in image tag for cache invalidation.\n* **node-modules-isolation**: CONFIRMED — Standard Docker volume pattern. Critical for avoiding macOS vs Linux binary conflicts.\n\n## Requirements\n\n### Detection logic\n\nScan the project root for:\n\n| File | Provisions |\n| -- | -- |\n| `.nvmrc` / `.node-version` | Node.js (exact version) |\n| `pnpm-lock.yaml` | pnpm |\n| `package-lock.json` | npm |\n| `yarn.lock` | yarn |\n| `turbo.json` | turbo |\n| `nx.json` | nx |\n| `pyproject.toml` | Python + uv/pip |\n| `playwright.config.*` | Chromium + browser deps |\n\n### Image generation\n\n* Generate a Dockerfile in memory (or temp file), build via `std::process::Command` shelling out to `docker build`\n* Base image: `node:{version}-slim` (version from `.nvmrc`)\n* Install package manager via corepack\n* Install any detected task runners globally\n* Working directory: `/workspace`\n* No `COPY` — source comes via bind mount at runtime\n* Entrypoint: `sleep infinity` (container stays alive, commands come via `docker exec`)\n\n### Image naming and caching\n\n* Image name: `dual-{project}:latest`\n* All workspaces of the same project share the same image\n* Cache key: hash of `.nvmrc` + lockfile name + detected tooling\n* Only rebuild when cache key changes\n\n### CLI\n\n```\ndual image build <project>   — manually trigger image build\ndual image list              — show cached images\n```\n\nAutomatic: image builds on first `dual new` if no cached image exists.\n\n## Existing code\n\nExtend `src/container/` module from Phase 0 (PR #105).\n\n## Dependencies\n\n* Docker Spike validated ([ARCHITECTURE.md](<http://ARCHITECTURE.md>) 27/27) — no blockers remaining","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker {"id":"c79a976b-5117-41e9-adbe-8a802a50bb28","identifier":"DUAL-25","title":"Error handling and recovery","description":"## Description\n\nHandle failure modes gracefully across the workspace lifecycle. Dual is a tool used dozens of times daily — error paths need to be as solid as the happy path.\n\n## Architecture Validation\n\n* **error-message-transparency**: CONFIRMED WITH CAVEATS — Most leaks fixable with proper container config. Missing mounts cause OCI runtime errors (fix: complete mounts).\n\n## Failure scenarios to handle\n\n### Docker not available\n\n* Docker daemon not running → clear message: \"Docker Desktop is not running. Start it and try again.\"\n* Docker not installed → clear message with install link\n* Detect on startup, not on first container operation\n\n### Container failures\n\n* Image build fails (network error, invalid detection) → show build output, suggest retry\n* Container fails to start (port conflict on ephemeral range, OOM) → show Docker error, clean up partial state\n* `pnpm install` fails inside container → show error output, leave container running for debugging via `dual run`\n\n### Partial state cleanup\n\n* Clone created but container creation failed → `dual rm` should handle this (delete clone, skip container cleanup)\n* Container created but tmux session failed → `dual rm` should handle (destroy container, delete clone)\n* Orphan detection: on startup, scan for `dual-*` containers that don't match any known workspace → warn and offer cleanup\n\n### Port conflicts\n\n* Ephemeral host port already in use → try next available port in range\n* All ports in range exhausted → error with guidance\n* Non-Dual process occupying a port that the proxy wants → warn with PID/process name\n\n### Network issues\n\n* `git clone` fails (no network, auth failure) → show git error, no cleanup needed\n* Image pull fails (registry unreachable) → show error, suggest checking network\n\n### Recovery commands\n\n* `dual doctor` — diagnose common issues: Docker running? tmux installed? orphaned containers? port conflicts?\n* `dual cleanup` — remove orphaned containers and stale state\n\n## Existing code\n\nUse Rust's `thiserror` for typed errors and `anyhow` for error propagation. Error handling should be centralized via error types in each module. Every error message should be actionable.","projectMilestone":{"id":"6f559a1b-a290-4fc1-b9f5-3e9bcf64ade5","name":"Phase 1 — Docker Layer"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/jps0000-dual/issue/DUAL-25/error-handling-and-recovery","gitBranchName":"feature/dual-25-error-handling-and-recovery","createdAt":"2026-02-04T12:45:50.761Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-15T10:39:41.588Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-02-15T10:39:41.570Z","dueDate":null,"status":"Done","labels":["DX","Core"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Jeevan Pillay","createdById":"d75af8ad-93c1-4ecb-88d4-6913d56f5c85","project":"Dual","projectId":"e74e6d84-8867-44f7-9a71-dbc71f0d9bfe","team":"dual","teamId":"0aeeaf19-2b41-432d-80bb-ee55421894d5"}

  "old_string": "/// Build the git clone command arguments (for testing/debugging without executing).\npub fn build_clone_args(url: &str, branch: &str, target: &Path) -> Vec<String> {\n    let mut args = vec![\"clone\".to_string()];\n\n    if is_local_path(url) {\n        args.push(\"--local\".to_string());\n    }\n\n    args.push(\"-b\".to_string());\n    args.push(branch.to_string());\n    args.push(url.to_string());\n    args.push(target.to_string_lossy().to_string());\n\n    args\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Clone from a local main workspace using --local (hardlinks), then create a new branch.\n///\n/// This avoids the problem of `git clone -b <branch>` failing when the branch\n/// doesn't exist at the remote. Instead, we clone from the local main workspace\n/// and create the branch locally.\npub fn clone_from_local(\n    main_workspace_path: &Path,\n    target_dir: &Path,\n    new_branch: &str,\n) -> Result<PathBuf, CloneError> {\n    // Don't re-clone if it already exists\n    if target_dir.join(\".git\").exists() {\n        return Ok(target_dir.to_path_buf());\n    }\n\n    // Ensure parent directory exists\n    if let Some(parent) = target_dir.parent() {\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)\n            .map_err(|e| CloneError::Filesystem(parent.to_path_buf(), e))?;\n    }\n\n    // Step 1: git clone --local <main_workspace_path> <target_dir>\n    let clone_output = Command::new(\"git\")\n        .args(build_local_clone_args(main_workspace_path, target_dir))\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| CloneError::GitNotFound(e.to_string()))?;\n\n    if !clone_output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&clone_output.stderr).to_string();\n        return Err(CloneError::GitFailed {\n            repo: main_workspace_path.to_string_lossy().to_string(),\n            branch: new_branch.to_string(),\n            stderr,\n        });\n    }\n\n    // Step 2: git checkout -b <new_branch> in the cloned directory\n    let checkout_output = Command::new(\"git\")\n        .args([\"checkout\", \"-b\", new_branch])\n        .current_dir(target_dir)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| CloneError::GitNotFound(e.to_string()))?;\n\n    if !checkout_output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&checkout_output.stderr).to_string();\n        return Err(CloneError::GitFailed {\n            repo: main_workspace_path.to_string_lossy().to_string(),\n            branch: new_branch.to_string(),\n            stderr,\n        });\n    }\n\n    Ok(target_dir.to_path_buf())\n}\n\n/// Build the git clone --local arguments (for testing).\npub fn build_local_clone_args(main_workspace_path: &Path, target: &Path) -> Vec<String> {\n    vec![\n        \"clone\".to_string(),\n        \"--local\".to_string(),\n        main_workspace_path.to_string_lossy().to_string(),\n        target.to_string_lossy().to_string(),\n    ]\n}\n\n/// Build the git clone command arguments (for testing/debugging without executing).\npub fn build_clone_args(url: &str, branch: &str, target: &Path) -> Vec<String> {\n    let mut args = vec![\"clone\".to_string()];\n\n    if is_local_path(url) {\n        args.push(\"--local\".to_string());\n    }\n\n    args.push(\"-b\".to_string());\n    args.push(branch.to_string());\n    args.push(url.to_string());\n    args.push(target.to_string_lossy().to_string());\n\n    args\n}"
}

     8→use crate::config;
     9→
    10→const STATE_DIR: &str = ".dual";
    11→const STATE_FILENAME: &str = "workspaces.toml";
    12→const DEFAULT_WORKSPACE_ROOT: &str = ".dual/workspaces";
    13→
    14→#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Clone)]
    15→pub struct WorkspaceState {
    16→    /// Root directory for all workspace clones (default: ~/.dual/workspaces)
    17→    pub workspace_root: Option<String>,
    18→
    19→    /// Active workspace entries
    20→    #[serde(default)]
    21→    pub workspaces: Vec<WorkspaceEntry>,
    22→}
    23→
    24→#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Clone)]
    25→pub struct WorkspaceEntry {
    26→    /// Short repo name (e.g. "lightfast")
    27→    pub repo: String,
    28→
    29→    /// Git URL or local path
    30→    pub url: String,
    31→
    32→    /// Branch name (e.g. "main", "feat/auth")
    33→    pub branch: String,
    34→
    35→    /// Explicit path to workspace directory (for `dual add` — user's existing clone).
    36→    /// If None, workspace lives at {workspace_root}/{repo}/{encoded_branch}/
    37→    /// and will be cloned on first launch.
    38→    pub path: Option<String>,
    39→}
    40→
    41→impl WorkspaceState {
    42→    /// Create a new empty state.
    43→    pub fn new() -> Self {
    44→        Self {
    45→            workspace_root: None,
    46→            workspaces: Vec::new(),
    47→        }
    48→    }
    49→
    50→    /// Resolve the workspace root directory as an absolute path.
    51→    /// Uses the configured value or defaults to ~/.dual/workspaces.
    52→    pub fn workspace_root(&self) -> PathBuf {
    53→        if let Some(ref root) = self.workspace_root {
    54→            let expanded = shellexpand(root);
    55→            PathBuf::from(expanded)
    56→        } else {
    57→            dirs::home_dir()
    58→                .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from("."))
    59→                .join(DEFAULT_WORKSPACE_ROOT)
    60→        }
    61→    }
    62→
    63→    /// Get the workspace directory for an entry.
    64→    /// Uses explicit path if set, otherwise computes from workspace_root.
    65→    pub fn workspace_dir(&self, entry: &WorkspaceEntry) -> PathBuf {
    66→        if let Some(ref path) = entry.path {
    67→            PathBuf::from(shellexpand(path))
    68→        } else {
    69→            config::workspace_dir(&self.workspace_root(), &entry.repo, &entry.branch)
    70→        }
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    /// Find a workspace entry by identifier (e.g. "lightfast-main").
    74→    pub fn resolve_workspace(&self, identifier: &str) -> Option<&WorkspaceEntry> {
    75→        self.workspaces.iter().find(|ws| {
    76→            let id = config::workspace_id(&ws.repo, &ws.branch);
    77→            id == identifier
    78→        })
    79→    }
    80→
    81→    /// Get all workspace entries.
    82→    pub fn all_workspaces(&self) -> &[WorkspaceEntry] {
    83→        &self.workspaces
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    /// Add a workspace entry. Returns Err if duplicate exists.
    87→    pub fn add_workspace(&mut self, entry: WorkspaceEntry) -> Result<(), StateError> {
    88→        if self.has_workspace(&entry.repo, &entry.branch) {
    89→            return Err(StateError::DuplicateWorkspace(
    90→                entry.repo.clone(),
    91→                entry.branch.clone(),
    92→            ));
    93→        }
    94→        self.workspaces.push(entry);
    95→        Ok(())
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    /// Remove a workspace entry by repo + branch. Returns true if found.
    99→    pub fn remove_workspace(&mut self, repo: &str, branch: &str) -> bool {
   100→        let before = self.workspaces.len();
   101→        self.workspaces
   102→            .retain(|ws| !(ws.repo == repo && ws.branch == branch));
   103→        self.workspaces.len() < before
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    /// Find all workspaces for a given repo name.
   107→    pub fn workspaces_for_repo(&self, repo: &str) -> Vec<&WorkspaceEntry> {
   108→        self.workspaces
   109→            .iter()
   110→            .filter(|ws| ws.repo == repo)
   111→            .collect()
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    /// Check if a workspace entry exists for repo + branch.
   115→    pub fn has_workspace(&self, repo: &str, branch: &str) -> bool {
   116→        self.workspaces
   117→            .iter()
   118→            .any(|ws| ws.repo == repo && ws.branch == branch)
   119→    }
   120→}
   121→
   122→impl Default for WorkspaceState {
   123→    fn default() -> Self {
   124→        Self::new()
   125→    }
   126→}
   127→
   128→/// Get the state file path: ~/.dual/workspaces.toml
   129→pub fn state_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
   130→    dirs::home_dir().map(|home| home.join(STATE_DIR).join(STATE_FILENAME))
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Load state from default location. Returns empty state if file doesn't exist.
   134→pub fn load() -> Result<WorkspaceState, StateError> {
   135→    let path = state_path().ok_or(StateError::NoHomeDir)?;
   136→
   137→    if !path.exists() {
   138→        return Ok(WorkspaceState::new());
   139→    }
   140→
   141→    let contents =
   142→        std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|e| StateError::ReadError(path.clone(), e))?;
   143→    let state: WorkspaceState =
   144→        toml::from_str(&contents).map_err(|e| StateError::ParseError(path.clone(), e))?;
   145→    validate(&state)?;
   146→    debug!(count = state.workspaces.len(), "loaded state");
   147→    Ok(state)
   148→}
   149→
   150→/// Load state from a specific path.
   151→pub fn load_from(path: &Path) -> Result<WorkspaceState, StateError> {
   152→    if !path.exists() {
   153→        return Ok(WorkspaceState::new());
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    let contents =
   157→        std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|e| StateError::ReadError(path.to_path_buf(), e))?;
   158→    let state: WorkspaceState =
   159→        toml::from_str(&contents).map_err(|e| StateError::ParseError(path.to_path_buf(), e))?;
   160→    validate(&state)?;
   161→    Ok(state)
   162→}
   163→
   164→/// Save state to default location. Uses atomic write with backup.
   165→pub fn save(state: &WorkspaceState) -> Result<(), StateError> {
   166→    let path = state_path().ok_or(StateError::NoHomeDir)?;
   167→
   168→    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
   169→        fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(parent.to_path_buf(), e))?;
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    atomic_save(state, &path)
   173→}
   174→
   175→/// Save state to a specific path. Uses atomic write with backup.
   176→pub fn save_to(state: &WorkspaceState, path: &Path) -> Result<(), StateError> {
   177→    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
   178→        fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(parent.to_path_buf(), e))?;
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    atomic_save(state, path)
   182→}
   183→
   184→/// Atomic save: serialize -> write to temp file -> backup existing -> rename.
   185→///
   186→/// Uses advisory file locking to prevent concurrent writes.
   187→/// The lock is held on a lockfile for the duration of the write-backup-rename sequence.
   188→fn atomic_save(state: &WorkspaceState, path: &Path) -> Result<(), StateError> {
   189→    let contents = toml::to_string_pretty(state).map_err(StateError::SerializeError)?;
   190→
   191→    let lock_path = path.with_extension("lock");
   192→
   193→    // Acquire advisory lock
   194→    let lock_file =
   195→        File::create(&lock_path).map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(lock_path.clone(), e))?;
   196→    lock_file
   197→        .lock_exclusive()
   198→        .map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(lock_path.clone(), e))?;
   199→
   200→    // Write to temp file in the same directory (same filesystem for rename)
   201→    let tmp_path = path.with_extension("tmp");
   202→    let mut tmp_file =
   203→        File::create(&tmp_path).map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(tmp_path.clone(), e))?;
   204→    tmp_file
   205→        .write_all(contents.as_bytes())
   206→        .map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(tmp_path.clone(), e))?;
   207→    tmp_file
   208→        .sync_all()
   209→        .map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(tmp_path.clone(), e))?;
   210→
   211→    // Backup existing file (best-effort — don't fail if original doesn't exist)
   212→    let bak_path = path.with_extension("toml.bak");
   213→    if path.exists() {
   214→        let _ = fs::copy(path, &bak_path);
   215→    }
   216→
   217→    // Atomic rename (POSIX guarantees this is atomic on same filesystem)
   218→    fs::rename(&tmp_path, path).map_err(|e| StateError::WriteError(path.to_path_buf(), e))?;
   219→
   220→    // Release lock (dropped with file, but explicit for clarity)
   221→    let _ = lock_file.unlock();
   222→    let _ = fs::remove_file(&lock_path);
   223→
   224→    Ok(())
   225→}
   226→
   227→/// Parse state from TOML string (for testing).
   228→pub fn parse(toml_str: &str) -> Result<WorkspaceState, StateError> {
   229→    let state: WorkspaceState = toml::from_str(toml_str)
   230→        .map_err(|e| StateError::ParseError(PathBuf::from("<string>"), e))?;
   231→    validate(&state)?;
   232→    Ok(state)
   233→}
   234→
   235→fn validate(state: &WorkspaceState) -> Result<(), StateError> {
   236→    for (i, ws) in state.workspaces.iter().enumerate() {
   237→        if ws.repo.is_empty() {
   238→            return Err(StateError::Validation(format!(
   239→                "workspaces[{i}]: 'repo' cannot be empty"
   240→            )));
   241→        }
   242→        if ws.url.is_empty() {
   243→            return Err(StateError::Validation(format!(
   244→                "workspaces[{i}] ({}): 'url' cannot be empty",
   245→                ws.repo
   246→            )));
   247→        }
   248→        if ws.branch.is_empty() {
   249→            return Err(StateError::Validation(format!(
   250→                "workspaces[{i}] ({}): 'branch' cannot be empty",
   251→                ws.repo
   252→            )));
   253→        }
   254→    }
   255→    Ok(())
   256→}
   257→
   258→/// Minimal ~ expansion for workspace_root paths.
   259→fn shellexpand(path: &str) -> String {
   260→    if let Some(rest) = path.strip_prefix("~/")
   261→        && let Some(home) = dirs::home_dir()
   262→    {
   263→        return home.join(rest).to_string_lossy().into_owned();
   264→    }
   265→    path.to_string()
   266→}
   267→
   268→#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
   269→pub enum StateError {
   270→    #[error("Could not determine home directory")]
   271→    NoHomeDir,
   272→
   273→    #[error("Failed to read {path}: {err}", path = .0.display(), err = .1)]
   274→    ReadError(PathBuf, std::io::Error),
   275→
   276→    #[error("Failed to write {path}: {err}", path = .0.display(), err = .1)]
   277→    WriteError(PathBuf, std::io::Error),
   278→
   279→    #[error("Failed to parse {path}: {err}", path = .0.display(), err = .1)]
   280→    ParseError(PathBuf, toml::de::Error),
   281→
   282→    #[error("Failed to serialize state: {0}")]
   283→    SerializeError(toml::ser::Error),
   284→
   285→    #[error("Invalid state: {0}")]
   286→    Validation(String),
   287→
   288→    #[error("Workspace {0}/{1} already exists")]
   289→    DuplicateWorkspace(String, String),
   290→}

   310→    #[test]
   311→    fn parse_state_with_workspaces() {
   312→        let toml = r#"
   313→workspace_root = "~/my-workspaces"
   314→
   315→[[workspaces]]
   316→repo = "lightfast"
   317→url = "git@github.com:org/lightfast.git"
   318→branch = "main"
   319→path = "/Users/dev/code/lightfast"
   320→
   321→[[workspaces]]
   322→repo = "lightfast"
   323→url = "git@github.com:org/lightfast.git"
   324→branch = "feat/auth"
   325→
   326→[[workspaces]]
   327→repo = "agent-os"
   328→url = "/local/path/to/agent-os"
   329→branch = "main"
   330→path = "/local/path/to/agent-os"
   331→"#;
   332→        let state = parse(toml).unwrap();
   333→        assert_eq!(state.workspace_root.as_deref(), Some("~/my-workspaces"));
   334→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces.len(), 3);
   335→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces[0].repo, "lightfast");
   336→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces[0].branch, "main");
   337→        assert_eq!(
   338→            state.workspaces[0].path.as_deref(),
   339→            Some("/Users/dev/code/lightfast")
   340→        );
   341→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces[1].repo, "lightfast");
   342→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces[1].branch, "feat/auth");
   343→        assert!(state.workspaces[1].path.is_none());
   344→        assert_eq!(state.workspaces[2].repo, "agent-os");
   345→    }

   638→    #[test]
   639→    fn save_and_load_roundtrip() {
   640→        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("dual-test-state-roundtrip");
   641→        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
   642→        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
   643→        let path = dir.join("workspaces.toml");
   644→
   645→        let mut state = WorkspaceState::new();
   646→        state.workspace_root = Some("/tmp/ws".to_string());
   647→        state
   648→            .add_workspace(WorkspaceEntry {
   649→                repo: "test".to_string(),
   650→                url: "https://example.com/test.git".to_string(),
   651→                branch: "main".to_string(),
   652→                path: Some("/home/user/test".to_string()),
   653→            })
   654→            .unwrap();
   655→
   656→        save_to(&state, &path).unwrap();
   657→        let loaded = load_from(&path).unwrap();
   658→        assert_eq!(state, loaded);
   659→

  "new_string": "/// Write a default .dual.toml with helpful comments and examples.\n/// Used by `dual add` when creating a new repo config.\npub fn write_default_hints(repo_root: &Path) -> Result<(), HintsError> {\n    let template = r#\"# Dual workspace configuration\n# See: https://github.com/jeevanpillay/dual\n\n# Docker image for the container runtime\nimage = \"node:20\"\n\n# Ports your dev server uses (for reverse proxy routing)\n# Example: ports = [3000, 3001]\n# ports = []\n\n# Shell command to run after container creation (e.g., dependency install)\n# Example: setup = \"pnpm install\"\n# setup = \"\"\n\n# Environment variables passed to the container\n# Example:\n# [env]\n# NODE_ENV = \"development\"\n\n# Commands to route to the container (in addition to defaults)\n# Default: npm, npx, pnpm, node, python, python3, pip, pip3, curl, make\n# Example: extra_commands = [\"cargo\", \"go\", \"ruby\"]\n# extra_commands = []\n\n# Directories to isolate with anonymous Docker volumes\n# These directories get their own volume so they don't sync between host/container\n# Example: anonymous_volumes = [\"node_modules\", \".next\", \"target\"]\n# anonymous_volumes = [\"node_modules\"]\n\n# Files to share across all workspaces of this repo\n# These are gitignored files that should be available in every branch workspace\n# [shared]\n# files = [\".env.local\", \".vercel\"]\n\"#;\n    let hints_path = repo_root.join(HINTS_FILENAME);\n    std::fs::write(&hints_path, template)\n        .map_err(|e| HintsError::WriteError(hints_path, e))?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Parse hints from TOML string (for testing).\npub fn parse_hints(toml_str: &str) -> Result<RepoHints, HintsError> {"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@jeevanpillaystudios/dual/src/container.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Build the docker create arguments (for testing).\npub fn build_create_args(name: &str, workspace_dir: &Path, image: &str) -> Vec<String> {\n    vec![\n        \"create\".to_string(),\n        \"--name\".to_string(),\n        name.to_string(),\n        // Bind mount workspace\n        \"-v\".to_string(),\n        format!(\"{}:{WORKSPACE_MOUNT}\", workspace_dir.display()),\n        // Anonymous volume for node_modules isolation\n        \"-v\".to_string(),\n        format!(\"{WORKSPACE_MOUNT}/node_modules\"),\n        // Working directory\n        \"-w\".to_string(),\n        WORKSPACE_MOUNT.to_string(),\n        // Image\n        image.to_string(),\n        // Keep container running for docker exec\n        \"sleep\".to_string(),\n        \"infinity\".to_string(),\n    ]\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Execute a setup command inside a running container.\n///\n/// Runs `docker exec <name> sh -c \"<setup_cmd>\"` and waits for completion.\npub fn exec_setup(name: &str, setup_cmd: &str) -> Result<(), ContainerError> {\n    let output = Command::new(\"docker\")\n        .args(build_exec_setup_args(name, setup_cmd))\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| ContainerError::DockerNotFound(e.to_string()))?;\n\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).to_string();\n        return Err(ContainerError::Failed {\n            operation: \"exec setup\".to_string(),\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            stderr,\n        });\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Build docker exec setup arguments (for testing).\npub fn build_exec_setup_args(name: &str, setup_cmd: &str) -> Vec<String> {\n    vec![\n        \"exec\".to_string(),\n        \"-w\".to_string(),\n        WORKSPACE_MOUNT.to_string(),\n        name.to_string(),\n        \"sh\".to_string(),\n        \"-c\".to_string(),\n        setup_cmd.to_string(),\n    ]\n}\n\n/// Build the docker create arguments (for testing).\npub fn build_create_args(\n    name: &str,\n    workspace_dir: &Path,\n    image: &str,\n    env: &HashMap<String, String>,\n    anonymous_volumes: &[String],\n) -> Vec<String> {\n    let mut args = vec![\n        \"create\".to_string(),\n        \"--name\".to_string(),\n        name.to_string(),\n        // Bind mount workspace\n        \"-v\".to_string(),\n        format!(\"{}:{WORKSPACE_MOUNT}\", workspace_dir.display()),\n    ];\n\n    // Anonymous volumes for directory isolation\n    for vol in anonymous_volumes {\n        args.push(\"-v\".to_string());\n        args.push(format!(\"{WORKSPACE_MOUNT}/{vol}\"));\n    }\n\n    // Environment variables\n    for (key, value) in env {\n        args.push(\"-e\".to_string());\n        args.push(format!(\"{key}={value}\"));\n    }\n\n    // Working directory\n    args.push(\"-w\".to_string());\n    args.push(WORKSPACE_MOUNT.to_string());\n\n    // Image\n    args.push(image.to_string());\n\n    // Keep container running for docker exec\n    args.push(\"sleep\".to_string());\n    args.push(\"infinity\".to_string());\n\n    args\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@jeevanpillaystudios/dual/src/shell.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Generate shell RC content that intercepts runtime commands for a workspace.\n///\n/// The generated content defines shell functions that wrap `docker exec`\n/// for each command that should route to the container. These functions\n/// handle TTY detection automatically.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n/// * `container_name` - The Docker container name (e.g. \"dual-lightfast-main\")\npub fn generate_rc(container_name: &str) -> String {\n    let mut rc = String::new();\n\n    rc.push_str(\"# Generated by dual — do not edit\\n\");\n    rc.push_str(&format!(\n        \"# Shell interceptors for container: {container_name}\\n\\n\"\n    ));\n\n    // Set the container name as an environment variable for reference\n    rc.push_str(&format!(\"export DUAL_CONTAINER=\\\"{container_name}\\\"\\n\\n\"));\n\n    // Generate a function for each container command\n    for cmd in CONTAINER_COMMANDS {\n        rc.push_str(&generate_function(cmd, container_name));\n        rc.push('\\n');\n    }\n\n    rc\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Generate shell RC content that intercepts runtime commands for a workspace.\n///\n/// The generated content defines shell functions that wrap `docker exec`\n/// for each command that should route to the container. These functions\n/// handle TTY detection automatically.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n/// * `container_name` - The Docker container name (e.g. \"dual-lightfast-main\")\n/// * `extra_commands` - Additional commands to route to the container\npub fn generate_rc(container_name: &str, extra_commands: &[String]) -> String {\n    let mut rc = String::new();\n\n    rc.push_str(\"# Generated by dual — do not edit\\n\");\n    rc.push_str(&format!(\n        \"# Shell interceptors for container: {container_name}\\n\\n\"\n    ));\n\n    // Set the container name as an environment variable for reference\n    rc.push_str(&format!(\"export DUAL_CONTAINER=\\\"{container_name}\\\"\\n\\n\"));\n\n    // Generate a function for each default container command\n    for cmd in CONTAINER_COMMANDS {\n        rc.push_str(&generate_function(cmd, container_name));\n        rc.push('\\n');\n    }\n\n    // Generate a function for each extra command\n    for cmd in extra_commands {\n        if !CONTAINER_COMMANDS.contains(&cmd.as_str()) {\n            rc.push_str(&generate_function(cmd, container_name));\n            rc.push('\\n');\n        }\n    }\n\n    rc\n}"
}

  "new_string": "    // Step 3: Ensure container exists and is running\n    let is_new_container = matches!(container::status(&container_name), container::ContainerStatus::Missing);\n    match container::status(&container_name) {\n        container::ContainerStatus::Missing => {\n            info!(\"Creating container {container_name}...\");\n            if let Err(e) = container::create(\n                &container_name,\n                &workspace_dir,\n                &hints.image,\n                &hints.env,\n                &hints.anonymous_volumes,\n            ) {\n                error!(\"container create failed: {e}\");\n                return 1;\n            }\n            if let Err(e) = container::start(&container_name) {\n                error!(\"container start failed: {e}\");\n                return 1;\n            }\n        }\n        container::ContainerStatus::Stopped => {\n            info!(\"Starting container {container_name}...\");\n            if let Err(e) = container::start(&container_name) {\n                error!(\"container start failed: {e}\");\n                return 1;\n            }\n        }\n        container::ContainerStatus::Running => {}\n    }\n\n    // Step 3.5: Run setup command on new containers\n    if is_new_container {\n        if let Some(ref setup) = hints.setup {\n            info!(\"Running setup: {setup}\");\n            if let Err(e) = container::exec_setup(&container_name, setup) {\n                error!(\"setup failed: {e}\");\n                return 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Step 4: Write shell RC file\n    let rc_path = match shell::write_rc_file(&container_name, &hints.extra_commands) {\n        Ok(p) => p,\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"failed to write shell RC: {e}\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    };"
}

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn generate_rc_contains_functions() {\n        let rc = generate_rc(\"dual-lightfast-main\", &[]);\n\n        // Should contain the container name export\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"export DUAL_CONTAINER=\\\"dual-lightfast-main\\\"\"));\n\n        // Should contain functions for all container commands\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"npm()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"npx()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"pnpm()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"node()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"python()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"curl()\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn generate_rc_has_tty_detection() {\n        let rc = generate_rc(\"dual-lightfast-main\", &[]);\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"if [ -t 1 ]; then\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"docker exec -t\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn generate_rc_uses_correct_container() {\n        let rc = generate_rc(\"dual-agent-os-feat__memory\", &[]);\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"dual-agent-os-feat__memory\"));\n        // Every function should reference this container\n        for cmd in CONTAINER_COMMANDS {\n            assert!(rc.contains(&format!(\n                \"command docker exec -t -w /workspace dual-agent-os-feat__memory {cmd}\"\n            )));\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn generate_rc_with_extra_commands() {\n        let extras = vec![\"cargo\".to_string(), \"go\".to_string()];\n        let rc = generate_rc(\"dual-test\", &extras);\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"cargo()\"));\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"go()\"));\n        // Default commands should still be present\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"npm()\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn generate_rc_extra_commands_no_duplicates() {\n        // If an extra command matches a default, it should not be duplicated\n        let extras = vec![\"npm\".to_string(), \"cargo\".to_string()];\n        let rc = generate_rc(\"dual-test\", &extras);\n        let npm_count = rc.matches(\"npm()\").count();\n        assert_eq!(npm_count, 1);\n        assert!(rc.contains(\"cargo()\"));\n    }"
}

     1→/// E2E integration tests for Dual.
     2→///
     3→/// These tests require Docker and tmux to be available on the system.
     4→/// Run with: `cargo test --test e2e`
     5→///
     6→/// Tests are marked `#[ignore]` so they don't run during `cargo test` (which runs
     7→/// unit tests only). Run ignored tests with: `cargo test --test e2e -- --ignored`
     8→/// Or run all tests including ignored: `cargo test --test e2e -- --include-ignored`

    17→fn clone_creates_workspace() {
    18→    let mut f = harness::TestFixture::new();
    19→    let temp = f.temp_dir();
    20→    let repo_dir = fixtures::create_fixture_repo(&temp);
    21→    let workspace_root = temp.join("workspaces");
    22→
    23→    let state = fixtures::fixture_state(&workspace_root, &repo_dir, "test-app", "main");
    24→    let ws_root = state.workspace_root();
    25→
    26→    let clone_dir =
    27→        dual::clone::clone_workspace(&ws_root, "test-app", &repo_dir.to_string_lossy(), "main")
    28→            .expect("clone should succeed");
    29→
    30→    assert!(clone_dir.exists());
    31→    assert!(clone_dir.join(".git").exists());
    32→    assert!(clone_dir.join("package.json").exists());
    33→    assert!(clone_dir.join("server.js").exists());
    34→}

    77→fn container_lifecycle() {
    78→    let mut f = harness::TestFixture::new();
    79→    let temp = f.temp_dir();
    80→    let repo_dir = fixtures::create_fixture_repo(&temp);
    81→    let workspace_root = temp.join("workspaces");
    82→
    83→    let state = fixtures::fixture_state(&workspace_root, &repo_dir, "test-app", "main");
    84→    let ws_root = state.workspace_root();
    85→    let url = repo_dir.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    86→
    87→    // Clone workspace first (container needs bind mount target)
    88→    dual::clone::clone_workspace(&ws_root, "test-app", &url, "main").expect("clone should succeed");
    89→
    90→    // Use test-prefixed container name
    91→    let container_name = f.container_name();
    92→    f.register_container(container_name.clone());
    93→
    94→    // Create container with explicit name
    95→    let workspace_dir = dual::config::workspace_dir(&ws_root, "test-app", "main");
    96→    let args = dual::container::build_create_args(&container_name, &workspace_dir, "node:20");
    97→    let output = Command::new("docker").args(&args).output().unwrap();
    98→    assert!(
    99→        output.status.success(),
   100→        "docker create failed: {}",
   101→        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
   102→    );
   103→
   104→    // Status should be Stopped (created but not started)
   105→    assert_eq!(
   106→        dual::container::status(&container_name),
   107→        dual::container::ContainerStatus::Stopped
   108→    );
   109→
   110→    // Start
   111→    dual::container::start(&container_name).expect("start should succeed");
   112→    assert_eq!(
   113→        dual::container::status(&container_name),
   114→        dual::container::ContainerStatus::Running
   115→    );
   116→
   117→    // Stop
   118→    dual::container::stop(&container_name).expect("stop should succeed");
   119→    assert_eq!(
   120→        dual::container::status(&container_name),
   121→        dual::container::ContainerStatus::Stopped
   122→    );
   123→
   124→    // Destroy (remove)
   125→    dual::container::destroy(&container_name).expect("destroy should succeed");
   126→    assert_eq!(
   127→        dual::container::status(&container_name),
   128→        dual::container::ContainerStatus::Missing
   129→    );
   130→}

```
     1→/// Default commands that route to the container.
     2→const CONTAINER_COMMANDS: &[&str] = &[
     3→    "npm", "npx", "pnpm", "node", "python", "python3", "pip", "pip3", "curl", "make",
     4→];
     5→
     6→/// Where a command should execute.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
     8→pub enum RouteTarget {
     9→    Container,
    10→    Host,
    11→}
    12→
    13→/// Classify a command as host or container.
    14→///
    15→/// Default rules from SPEC.md:
    16→/// - npm/pnpm/node/python/curl etc → container
    17→/// - Everything else → host
    18→pub fn classify(command: &str) -> RouteTarget {
    19→    // Extract the base command name (strip path prefix)
    20→    let base = command.rsplit('/').next().unwrap_or(command);
    21→
    22→    if CONTAINER_COMMANDS.contains(&base) {
    23→        RouteTarget::Container
    24→    } else {
    25→        RouteTarget::Host
    26→    }
    27→}
    28→
    29→/// Generate shell RC content that intercepts runtime commands for a workspace.
    30→///
    31→/// The generated content defines shell functions that wrap `docker exec`
    32→/// for each command that should route to the container. These functions
    33→/// handle TTY detection automatically.
    34→///
    35→/// # Arguments
    36→/// * `container_name` - The Docker container name (e.g. "dual-lightfast-main")
    37→/// * `extra_commands` - Additional commands to route to the container
    38→pub fn generate_rc(container_name: &str, extra_commands: &[String]) -> String {
    39→    let mut rc = String::new();
    40→
    41→    rc.push_str("# Generated by dual — do not edit\n");
    42→    rc.push_str(&format!(
    43→        "# Shell interceptors for container: {container_name}\n\n"
    44→    ));
    45→
    46→    // Set the container name as an environment variable for reference
    47→    rc.push_str(&format!("export DUAL_CONTAINER=\"{container_name}\"\n\n"));
    48→
    49→    // Generate a function for each default container command
    50→    for cmd in CONTAINER_COMMANDS {
    51→        rc.push_str(&generate_function(cmd, container_name));
    52→        rc.push('\n');
    53→    }
    54→
    55→    // Generate a function for each extra command
    56→    for cmd in extra_commands {
    57→        if !CONTAINER_COMMANDS.contains(&cmd.as_str()) {
    58→            rc.push_str(&generate_function(cmd, container_name));
    59→            rc.push('\n');
    60→        }
    61→    }
    62→
    63→    rc
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Generate a single shell function that intercepts a command.
    67→///
    68→/// The function:
    69→/// - Detects if stdout is a TTY and passes -t to docker exec if so
    70→/// - Forwards all arguments to the command inside the container
    71→/// - Preserves exit codes
    72→fn generate_function(command: &str, container_name: &str) -> String {
    73→    format!(
    74→        r#"{command}() {{
    75→    if [ -t 1 ]; then
    76→        command docker exec -t -w /workspace {container_name} {command} "$@"
    77→    else
    78→        command docker exec -w /workspace {container_name} {command} "$@"
    79→    fi
    80→}}
    81→"#
    82→    )
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Generate a shell command to source the RC content from a string.
    86→/// Useful for injecting into tmux pane creation.
    87→pub fn source_command(container_name: &str) -> String {
    88→    format!("eval \"$(dual shell-rc {container_name})\"")
    89→}
    90→
    91→/// Write the shell RC to a file and return the path.
    92→/// RC files are written to ~/.config/dual/rc/{container_name}.sh
    93→pub fn write_rc_file(
    94→    container_name: &str,
    95→    extra_commands: &[String],
    96→) -> Result<std::path::PathBuf, std::io::Error> {
    97→    let rc_dir = dirs::config_dir()
    98→        .unwrap_or_else(|| std::path::PathBuf::from(".config"))
    99→        .join("dual")
   100→        .join("rc");
   101→    std::fs::create_dir_all(&rc_dir)?;
   102→
   103→    let rc_path = rc_dir.join(format!("{container_name}.sh"));
   104→    let rc_content = generate_rc(container_name, extra_commands);
   105→    std::fs::write(&rc_path, rc_content)?;
   106→
   107→    Ok(rc_path)
   108→}
   109→
   110→/// Get the source command for an RC file path.
   111→pub fn source_file_command(rc_path: &std::path::Path) -> String {
   112→    format!("source {}", rc_path.display())
   113→}
   114→
   115→#[cfg(test)]
   116→mod tests {
   117→    use super::*;
   118→
   119→    #[test]
   120→    fn classify_container_commands() {
   121→        assert_eq!(classify("npm"), RouteTarget::Container);
   122→        assert_eq!(classify("npx"), RouteTarget::Container);
   123→        assert_eq!(classify("pnpm"), RouteTarget::Container);
   124→        assert_eq!(classify("node"), RouteTarget::Container);
   125→        assert_eq!(classify("python"), RouteTarget::Container);
   126→        assert_eq!(classify("python3"), RouteTarget::Container);
   127→        assert_eq!(classify("pip"), RouteTarget::Container);
   128→        assert_eq!(classify("curl"), RouteTarget::Container);
   129→        assert_eq!(classify("make"), RouteTarget::Container);
   130→    }
   131→
   132→    #[test]
   133→    fn classify_host_commands() {
   134→        assert_eq!(classify("git"), RouteTarget::Host);
   135→        assert_eq!(classify("cat"), RouteTarget::Host);
   136→        assert_eq!(classify("ls"), RouteTarget::Host);
   137→        assert_eq!(classify("vim"), RouteTarget::Host);
   138→        assert_eq!(classify("nvim"), RouteTarget::Host);
   139→        assert_eq!(classify("ssh"), RouteTarget::Host);
   140→    }
   141→
   142→    #[test]
   143→    fn classify_strips_path_prefix() {
   144→        assert_eq!(classify("/usr/bin/npm"), RouteTarget::Container);
   145→        assert_eq!(classify("/usr/local/bin/node"), RouteTarget::Container);
   146→        assert_eq!(classify("/usr/bin/git"), RouteTarget::Host);
   147→    }
   148→
   149→    #[test]
   150→    fn generate_rc_contains_functions() {
   151→        let rc = generate_rc("dual-lightfast-main", &[]);
   152→
   153→        // Should contain the container name export
   154→        assert!(rc.contains("export DUAL_CONTAINER=\"dual-lightfast-main\""));
   155→
   156→        // Should contain functions for all container commands
   157→        assert!(rc.contains("npm()"));
   158→        assert!(rc.contains("npx()"));
   159→        assert!(rc.contains("pnpm()"));
   160→        assert!(rc.contains("node()"));
   161→        assert!(rc.contains("python()"));
   162→        assert!(rc.contains("curl()"));
   163→    }
   164→
   165→    #[test]
   166→    fn generate_rc_has_tty_detection() {
   167→        let rc = generate_rc("dual-lightfast-main", &[]);
   168→        assert!(rc.contains("if [ -t 1 ]; then"));
   169→        assert!(rc.contains("docker exec -t"));
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    #[test]
   173→    fn generate_rc_uses_correct_container() {
   174→        let rc = generate_rc("dual-agent-os-feat__memory", &[]);
   175→        assert!(rc.contains("dual-agent-os-feat__memory"));
   176→        // Every function should reference this container
   177→        for cmd in CONTAINER_COMMANDS {
   178→            assert!(rc.contains(&format!(
   179→                "command docker exec -t -w /workspace dual-agent-os-feat__memory {cmd}"
   180→            )));
   181→        }
   182→    }
   183→
   184→    #[test]
   185→    fn generate_rc_with_extra_commands() {
   186→        let extras = vec!["cargo".to_string(), "go".to_string()];
   187→        let rc = generate_rc("dual-test", &extras);
   188→        assert!(rc.contains("cargo()"));
   189→        assert!(rc.contains("go()"));
   190→        // Default commands should still be present
   191→        assert!(rc.contains("npm()"));
   192→    }
   193→
   194→    #[test]
   195→    fn generate_rc_extra_commands_no_duplicates() {
   196→        // If an extra command matches a default, it should not be duplicated
   197→        let extras = vec!["npm".to_string(), "cargo".to_string()];
   198→        let rc = generate_rc("dual-test", &extras);
   199→        // Count "npm() {" specifically to avoid matching "pnpm()" substring
   200→        let npm_count = rc.matches("npm() {").count();
   201→        assert_eq!(npm_count, 1);
   202→        assert!(rc.contains("cargo()"));
   203→    }
   204→
   205→    #[test]
   206→    fn generated_function_preserves_args() {
   207→        let func = generate_function("npm", "dual-test");
   208→        assert!(func.contains("\"$@\""));
   209→    }
   210→
   211→    #[test]
   212→    fn source_command_format() {
   213→        let cmd = source_command("dual-lightfast-main");
   214→        assert_eq!(cmd, "eval \"$(dual shell-rc dual-lightfast-main)\"");
   215→    }
   216→
   217→    #[test]
   218→    fn write_rc_file_creates_file() {
   219→        let path = write_rc_file("dual-test-write-rc", &[]).unwrap();
   220→        assert!(path.exists());
   221→        let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
   222→        assert!(content.contains("export DUAL_CONTAINER=\"dual-test-write-rc\""));
   223→        assert!(content.contains("npm()"));
   224→        // Clean up
   225→        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
   226→    }
   227→
   228→    #[test]
   229→    fn source_file_command_format() {
   230→        let path = std::path::Path::new("/home/user/.config/dual/rc/dual-test.sh");
   231→        let cmd = source_file_command(path);
   232→        assert_eq!(cmd, "source /home/user/.config/dual/rc/dual-test.sh");
   233→    }
   234→}
   235→

     1→# Dual v2.x → v3 Roadmap — Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→Incremental evolution from Dual v2.0.0 to v3.0.0. v2.x releases fix bugs, complete unimplemented features, and improve CLI ergonomics — all within the current architecture. v3.0.0 is the architectural rewrite (multiplexer trait, TUI, plugin system). This plan covers Linear project cleanup, every v2.x change, and v3 scoping.
     6→
     7→## Current State Analysis
     8→
     9→**v2.0.0 is shipped** (commit `3bb1635`). Working features:
    10→- CLI: add, create, launch, list, destroy, open, urls, sync, proxy, shell-rc
    11→- State management with atomic writes + file locking
    12→- Git clone lifecycle
    13→- Docker container lifecycle (create, start, stop, remove, status)
    14→- Shell command routing via RC file generation
    15→- Tmux session management
    16→- Reverse proxy with subdomain routing
    17→- Shared config propagation across workspaces
    18→- Production readiness (thiserror, atomic state, tracing)
    19→
    20→**Linear is stale**: Project still called "Dual v0", several shipped features show as "In Progress"
    21→
    22→### Key Discoveries:
    23→- `src/clone.rs:54` — `-b` flag requires branch to exist at origin (Bug 1)
    24→- `src/main.rs:669-688` — `detect_workspace()` exists but only used by `cmd_sync()` (Bug 2)
    25→- `src/config.rs:78-83` — `write_hints()` uses `toml::to_string_pretty()` which can't emit comments (Bug 3)
    26→- `src/main.rs:262` — `env` HashMap loaded then dropped, never passed to container
    27→- `src/config.rs:29` — `setup` field exists in schema, never executed
    28→- `src/shell.rs:2-4` — `CONTAINER_COMMANDS` is hardcoded, not configurable
    29→- `src/container.rs:156-157` — Anonymous `node_modules` volume is hardcoded
    30→- `src/tmux.rs:49-64` — `attach()` doesn't check `$TMUX` env var, creates nested sessions
    31→
    32→## Desired End State
    33→
    34→After v2.2.0:
    35→- All three bugs fixed (branch model, context detection, config defaults)
    36→- `env` and `setup` fields fully functional
    37→- Container commands configurable per-project
    38→- Anonymous volumes configurable per-project
    39→- Context-aware CLI (auto-detect repo from cwd)
    40→- Tmux nested session handling
    41→- Linear project cleaned up with proper milestones
    42→- Clear path to v3.0.0
    43→
    44→After v3.0.0:
    45→- Multiplexer trait with TmuxBackend (and ZellijBackend stub)
    46→- TUI as primary interface (`dual` with no args)
    47→- Session layout configuration
    48→- Plugin system for extensibility
    49→
    50→## What We're NOT Doing
    51→
    52→- **Not rewriting the module structure for v2.x** — v2.x fixes work within the current `main.rs`-orchestrated architecture
    53→- **Not adding TUI in v2.x** — TUI requires ratatui dependency and architectural changes (v3)
    54→- **Not abstracting Docker behind a trait in v2.x** — Podman support is v3+
    55→- **Not building a full plugin system in v2.x** — `.dual.toml` field additions are sufficient
    56→- **Not implementing ZellijBackend in v2.x** — multiplexer trait is v3
    57→- **Not adding session layout config in v2.x** — requires multiplexer trait (v3)
    58→
    59→## What Should Be Removed
    60→
    61→1. **Hardcoded `node_modules` volume** — Replace with configurable `anonymous_volumes` in `.dual.toml`
    62→2. **Stale Linear issues** — Close shipped work, mark duplicates
    63→3. **"Dual v0" project name** — Rename to "Dual"
    64→4. **Dead code paths** — `env` loading that goes nowhere (`main.rs:262`)
    65→
    66→---
    67→
    68→## Phase 0: Linear Project Cleanup
    69→
